Hi, yeah, thank you so much for seeing me. Um... I guess what really brought me in is just... I've been having a little bit of trouble with my focus lately? It's probably not a huge deal, but my mind just won’t stay on one thing anymore. 

I work as a jeweler, and when I'm at the bench, I'll kind of... I don't know, I'll jump from reading a customer email, to noticing a tiny saw mark on a piece, to some random noise outside. And then... well, it's a bit embarrassing to admit, but I'll realize I’ve completely lost track of the whole work order. I'm really trying my best to stay on top of my projects, I promise, it's just getting a little frustrating. Does that... does that make sense for why I'd come in?

Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm completely exhausted or anything. I mean, I'm still getting all my orders done on time! I'd never let my boss down like that. 

But... I guess I have been feeling a little more worn out than usual? Like, when I'm sitting at the bench, my arms just feel so heavy... almost like lead. And doing simple stuff, like polishing a setting or just carrying a tray of stones from the back, it just... it wipes me out. Even if I've had a full night off, I still feel like I've been working a double shift. 

I don't want to sound lazy, though! I'm really not a lazy person, I promise. It's just taking a little more effort to get through the day right now. That's pretty common when you work with your hands, right? I'm sure it's just physical strain and I just need to stretch more or something.

I mean, I wouldn't ever want to sound ungrateful for the things I have in my life, you know? Like I said, I still really love my job and my friends. \n\nI guess... maybe there are moments where I'm just a little too tired to fully appreciate things right then and there? Like, if a buddy calls to grab a beer, I might not be jumping out of my seat with excitement, but I still go and I still try to be good company. I don't want to be a drag to be around. No, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I really don't want to sound like I'm just complaining or, you know, not appreciating what I have. Does that... does that make sense for why I'd come in?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed this same trouble with focus or getting distracted in other parts of your life, like at home, in conversations, or while driving?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, maybe a little bit? But nothing major, I promise. Like, I think I'm doing okay at home. I keep the apartment pretty clean and I remember to pay my bills on time. \n\nIn conversations, sometimes my mind wanders, but I always try to smile and nod so people don't think I'm being rude. I really don't want to upset anyone or make them feel like I'm not listening. And driving is fine! I'm a very careful driver. I hope that's the right answer? I just don't want you to think I'm falling apart everywhere, because I'm really trying hard to keep it all together.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to stay upbeat, you know? I don't want to be a burden or sound like I'm just complaining. But... I guess, if I'm being totally honest... these past couple of weeks have been a little hard. \n\nI've been waking up with this... this really heavy, hollow feeling? It's almost like the shine has just gone out of everything. I'm still going to work every day, I promise! I don't want you to think I'm slacking off. But... well, it's really embarrassing to admit this, but I've actually cried in my truck a few times before going in to open the shop. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds terrible and weak. I'm really trying my best to push past it and do a good job for my customers. Is it... is it okay that I'm feeling that way? I hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you usually enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t bring you as much pleasure as they used to?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know, I guess I'm still doing okay with most of my hobbies. I really don't want to sound like I'm just giving up on everything. \n\nI still try to see my friends when they invite me out, and I always make sure to smile and be good company so I don't bring the mood down. I don't want them to worry about me. I mean, maybe I'm a little quieter than usual, or... or maybe it takes a bit more effort to get excited about things? But I'm still participating, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting at home doing nothing. Is it normal to just need a little extra push sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when even the things you usually enjoy, like your work or time with friends, felt completely uninteresting or joyless to you?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not completely uninteresting. I mean, I really love my job. Working with the stones and the metals, it's... it's a good trade. I don't want you to think I'm ungrateful for the work I have, especially since the shop relies on me. \n\nMaybe... maybe I'm just a little tired lately? Like, I still go out with my friends and I still do my work, I just... I might not be, you know, the life of the party right now. But I still care about it all! I really do. I'm not just, like, totally numb to everything. I'm still trying hard to appreciate the good things. That makes sense, right? I'm just having a bit of a slow week, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like nothing, not even things you used to enjoy, could really make you happy or feel good lately?\n\nPatient: Um... no, no, not at all. I mean, I can still feel happy, definitely. Like, if a customer is really thrilled with a piece I finished for them, that still feels really good, you know? I don't want you to think I'm just this miserable person who can't be cheered up. \n\nI guess maybe the good feelings just don't stick around quite as long right now? Or maybe they're just a little quieter than they usually are. But I can still smile and laugh and appreciate things. I'm really trying my best to focus on the positive, I promise. I don't want to be a downer. It's totally normal to not feel 100% happy all the time, right? I'm sure I'll bounce back soon.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has felt low or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I wouldn't say I'm, like, totally depressed or anything. I don't want to make it sound worse than it is, because I'm still getting up and going to work every day. I'm really trying to push through it. \n\nBut... I guess these past couple weeks, I have been waking up with this... this heavy, sort of hollow feeling? It's kind of like the shine has just gone out of everything, you know? Like a piece of silver that's tarnished and just won't polish up. I... I did actually cry in my truck a few times before opening the shop recently. But I made sure to wipe my face and get it together before anyone saw me! I don't want my customers or my boss to think I can't handle my job. It's just a rough patch, right? Everyone gets a little down sometimes, don't they? I'm sure I'm just overreacting a bit.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you've been feeling unusually tired or low on energy most days, even when you haven't been doing anything physically or mentally exhausting?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm completely exhausted or anything. I mean, I'm still getting all my orders done on time! I'd never let my boss down like that. \n\nBut... I guess I have been feeling a little more worn out than usual? Like, when I'm sitting at the bench, my arms just feel so heavy... almost like lead. And doing simple stuff, like polishing a setting or just carrying a tray of stones from the back, it just... it wipes me out. Even if I've had a full night off, I still feel like I've been working a double shift. \n\nI don't want to sound lazy, though! I'm really not a lazy person, I promise. It's just taking a little more effort to get through the day right now. That's pretty common when you work with your hands, right? I'm sure it's just physical strain and I just need to stretch more or something.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that activities you usually enjoy, like working with stones or spending time with friends, feel less rewarding or enjoyable than they used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say that. I still really like working with the stones. Like, when I get to set a really nice sapphire or something, that still feels great. And hanging out with my friends is still fun, too. \n\nI mean, I might be a little tired, like I was saying before, but I'm definitely not losing interest in the things I love. I'd hate to be that guy who just stops caring about his work or blows off his buddies, you know? I'm really trying to keep up with everything and stay social so I don't worry anyone. It's good to keep busy and stay connected, isn't it? So, yeah, I definitely still enjoy all that stuff, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where even things you really love, like working with stones or seeing a close friend, just feel completely flat or empty, like they don’t bring you any joy at all?\n\nPatient: Um, no, definitely not completely flat. And with the stones, sometimes I just have to focus really hard on the technical part of the setting rather than how pretty it is, just to get it done right. \n\nBut it's not like I feel empty about it! I'm still trying my best to stay positive and appreciate everything. That's the right attitude to have, isn't it? Just kind of focusing on the good stuff, even if you're a bit worn out? So, no, I wouldn't say things feel completely flat at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like even the things you normally enjoy, like your work or time with friends, haven’t been able to bring you any pleasure at all, even for a little while?\n\nPatient: Um... no, no, not at all. Oh, man... I am so sorry. I feel like I'm really letting you down here. You keep having to ask me this, so I must be doing a terrible job of explaining myself, and I hate that I'm making your job harder. I really am trying to answer right, I promise. 

But... no, I honestly don't feel like things aren't worth the effort. Going into the shop, getting my hands on the metal, seeing my buddies... it still feels good. It's still completely worth it to me. 

I really hope I'm not coming across as stubborn or like I'm trying to hide something from you. Am I... am I supposed to be feeling like things aren't worth it? I just want to make sure I'm doing this the way you need me to. Are my answers okay?
